# Teams and Workflows, Plan C (UI and App Commands) Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** The person builds, runs and checks a team's workflows in the app: Teams in the rail, the team page, the builder with its step and trigger sheets, the run page, the chat card, the Inbox's "Workflows" section and the notifications; the app gets a command for every team, project, workflow and workflow-run action, and the contract and the UI's types gain them; and the person's decisions of 2026-11-04 on plan B's open questions are built.
**Architecture:** The core gets what the UI reads and the decisions: `report_outcome` and `send_back` never ask (`Policy::resolve_in`); the webhook caps its runs in flight (`server/workflow_webhook.rs`); a delegate step hands on its lead's branch plus the member branches it did not merge (`delegate::handed_branches`); a `push`, `pr` or `pr_merge` deploy with no branch fails (`deploy.rs`); the check is read before it is approved, the approval sends the tips it showed, and a branch's diff is from the default branch (`workflows/check.rs`, `workspace::range_diff`); workflow runs are listed by filter and by chat origin, a `Workflow` says its runs in flight and last run, and the builder checks a draft by the rules a save uses (`Teams::check_workflow`); waiting workflow runs are in the Inbox and in Needs you, and step runs stay out of Runs to review; a Test run starts while its workflow is off; a workflow run lists its steps' dot runs (`StepDotRun`). The app adds thin commands in `commands.rs`, contract samples in `contract/teams.rs`, and workflow notices in `events.rs`. The UI adds the schemas in `types.ts`, wrappers in `commands.ts`, keys in `query.ts`, invalidation in `runtime-events.ts`, and the screens in `ui/src/screens/teams/` (`builder/`, `run/`), with a hand-written `components/ui/sheet.tsx`. No migration.
**Tech Stack:** Rust (dots-core, dots-app on Tauri 2), React with TanStack Query v5 and zod 4 strict schemas, vitest with Testing Library. No new dependency.
**Spec:** docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-23-teams-workflows-design.md (sections 10 and 11; also 5.1, 5.4, 5.5, 6.2, 7 and 17 for the person's decisions of 2026-11-04 on plan B's open questions; Plans A and B did the core)

## Scope

This is the third of three plans for the spec:

- **Plan A (merged, PR #22):** teams, members, projects, workflows, workflow runs and step runs; `manual` and `chat` triggers; agent steps; checks (approve, stop); budget and clock; hand-offs, start branch and Dots' commit; `start_workflow`; the runner's workspace, exemption, restart and cancellation.
- **Plan B (merged, PR #55, as `bac99662` on 2026-11-04):** delegate steps and `start_member`, send-backs and their cap, `report_outcome` and `send_back`, deploys, branch steps, the `schedule` and `webhook` triggers, and plan A's parked findings. Its section 17 left three open questions and some known gaps.
- **Plan C (this one):** section 10 and the app commands and events of 11: Teams in the rail, the team page, the builder, the run page, the chat card, Needs you, the Inbox and the notifications; `Workflow`, `WorkflowRun`, `StepRun`, `Handoff` and the rest in the UI's types and the contract; the workflow webhook URL and a command for `Teams::regenerate_workflow_token`. Also the person's answers to plan B's open questions (decisions 1 to 3 below), the zero-branch gap (decision 4), Approve sending the tips the page showed (plan B's Task 8 finding), and a Test run that starts while its workflow is off (Task 10).

## Global Constraints

- Repository root `C:/Users/aksha/dots`, branch `feat/teams-c` (at `bac99662`, origin/master after PR #55, when this plan was written), worked in `.claude/worktrees/teams-c`; all commands run from the worktree root unless a step says otherwise (Git Bash). UI commands run in `ui/`, written as `(cd ui && …)`. Never commit to `master`. `git add` names its files: never `git add -A` or `git add .` (`.memsearch/` and the target folders are not ours). Conventional Commits, the subject each task gives; no `Co-Authored-By` line or any other AI trailer.
- Builds: `export CARGO_TARGET_DIR=C:/Users/aksha/dots/target-teams-c CARGO_INCREMENTAL=0`. Every cargo command takes `-j 2`, and every test run `-- --test-threads 4` (memory is short). Run one cargo or vitest command at a time, never two at once and never two in the background. Never share a target folder with another worktree. If a build is killed (low memory), do not start it again on your own: stop and ask the person. Building `dots-app` (the app tests and the workspace clippy run) needs `ui/dist`: once, `(cd ui && bun install && bun x vite build)`.
- UI: Bun via `bun` and `bun x`, never `bunx`. Tests: `(cd ui && DEBUG_PRINT_LIMIT=0 bun run test --maxWorkers=2 [files])`; without `--maxWorkers=2` the workers fail to start. Check the file count it reports against `git ls-files 'ui/src/**/*.test.ts' 'ui/src/**/*.test.tsx' | wc -l` plus the task's new test files. The typecheck, `(cd ui && bun run typecheck)`, runs after the tests. No formatter over whole UI files: match the surrounding style by hand. Deep Teal tokens only (`bg-needs`, `text-needs`, `border-needs-border`, `bg-needs-surface`, `bg-primary`, …): `lib/look-guard.test.ts` refuses palette colours. Every control has an accessible name (the tests find them by role and name).
- Never run the real `claude` CLI, the app or an installer, and never touch `%LOCALAPPDATA%\dots`. No network (no `shadcn add`, no package download). No new crate or npm dependency.
- No migration: no task needs a column (master has 0027).
- **Strict UI schemas.** The UI's zod schemas are `z.strictObject`. A change to a serialized type the UI parses (`Inbox`, a runtime event, anything in the contract) updates `ui/src/api/types.ts` and the samples in `apps/dots-app/src/contract.rs` (for teams, `contract/teams.rs`) in the same commit, then regenerates the fixture with `UPDATE_CONTRACT=1 cargo test -p dots-app contract -j 2 -- --test-threads 4`, then runs the app tests, the UI suite and the typecheck. A new list field goes into the empty literals of the UI tests and `ui/src/test/fixtures.ts` `installDefaults()`. A schema that `contract.test.ts` names before `types.ts` has it fails the whole file at load. The fixture `ui/src/api/__fixtures__/contract.json` has no code block below: the command writes it (Tasks 5, 6 and 15).
- The person's decisions (2026-11-04):
  1. Sandboxed and Custom stop asking for `report_outcome` and `send_back`. `start_member` and `start_workflow` still ask under Sandboxed and Custom. A dot's own `Workflow: deny` still refuses all four tools. The person's own `ask` was to be honoured if the policy can tell it from the preset's; it cannot, so `ask` counts as allow for these two tools only (a deviation, below).
  2. Spec 6.2 gets an ancestor rule. For a delegate step whose lead's own branch has commits, a check shows (and a deploy ships) the lead's branch plus every member branch whose tip is not an ancestor of the lead's tip (`git merge-base --is-ancestor`). A member branch that is gone is left out. Without lead commits, every member branch, as before.
  3. The webhook caps the runs in flight: 3 per workflow, waiting included (`MAX_WEBHOOK_IN_FLIGHT = 3`). Past it the webhook answers `429` with `too many runs in flight` and starts nothing. Checked after the token and the 409s, before the body is read.
  4. A `push`, `pr` or `pr_merge` deploy with zero approved branches fails with `Nothing to ship: the check approved no branch.`, and the workflow waits as for any failed step. `branch` mode with zero branches still succeeds.
  5. The Inbox lists every waiting workflow run (on a check, the budget, the send-back cap or a failed step) in a new section "Workflows", counted in the Inbox badge (UI `Layout.tsx` and core `inbox_count`). A run whose `origin_run_id` names a chat run also counts in that run's dot's `needs_you` (`dot_counts`). A run whose origin run was deleted (`origin_run_id` NULL) counts in the Inbox only.
  6. Step runs are quiet. A dot run with `workflow_step_run_id` gets no per-run "finished" or "failed" notification and is left out of "Runs to review" (`unreviewed_thread_ends`). Its questions and approvals notify and count as before.
  7. One plan C.
- Numbers, as the tests pin them: the webhook cap `MAX_WEBHOOK_IN_FLIGHT = 3` (runs `running` or `waiting`); the team page's Recent runs `RECENT_RUNS = 20`; the builder's live check debounced `CHECK_DELAY_MS = 300`; the sheets' limits `MAX_NAME = 80`, `MAX_JOB = 8_000`, `MAX_OUTCOME = 40`, `MAX_TASK = 8_000`; a new delegate `{ may_start: [], at_once: 3, total: 6 }` (`NEW_DELEGATE`); a new workflow's budget `DEFAULT_BUDGET` = 10 runs, 1,500,000 tokens, 120 minutes, 3 send-backs (the core's defaults); a notice's body is its first non-blank line, at most 200 characters; a step id `<kind>-<n>`, the smallest free n; the webhook URL `http://127.0.0.1:<port>/workflows/<id>/trigger`.

Look at the check again.` (several joined by `, `); `workflow run <id> is not waiting for a check`; `workflow run <id> shows no branch <branch>`; `workflow run <id> has no ready branch <branch>`; `folder <path> no longer exists`; `webhooks are off because the local server is not running: <reason>`; `workflow <name> is turned off`; `workflow <name> cannot start: <problem>`; `workflow run <id>` and `workflow <id>` (not found).

- How the code blocks are written. A step changes files in two ways, applied in the order given:
  - **Create `path`:** the block is the whole new file.
  - **In `path`, replace:** … **with:** … The first block occurs in the file exactly once, as it stands at that moment; the second takes its place.
- A Replace whose passage is not found once (someone changed it): stop, read the file, and apply the same change to the passage as it is now; never paste over their change. The working copy has CRLF line ends (`core.autocrlf=true`): a passage is matched with line ends ignored, and a file written here is LF with a final newline (git normalises it).

## Review Focus

The inputs and failure modes the spec implies that are most likely to bite a person, each pinned to the test that covers it:

1. **A step's run that sends `outcome` or `to_step` to pass as `report_outcome` or `send_back`.** Under Sandboxed and Custom, `report_outcome` and `send_back` go through without asking; `start_workflow` and `start_member` still ask, and a `start_*` call with decoy `outcome`, `to_step` or `note` arguments still asks (they are dropped before the gate). A `Workflow: deny`, or a default of deny, refuses all four. Tests: `workflow_tools_test::report_outcome_and_send_back_never_ask_and_a_deny_refuses_every_workflow_tool`, `a_sandboxed_step_reports_its_outcome_and_sends_back_without_asking`, `a_sandboxed_lead_asks_before_it_starts_a_member`; `local_tools_test::the_gate_asks_for_start_workflow_and_start_member_only` (Task 1).
2. **A branch that moves between the check page's render and the click, and a page that asks for a ref it was not shown.** Expected: Approve with tips git no longer has (or a missing, extra or repeated branch) is refused naming the branch, and nothing is recorded; the page reads the check again and the next Approve records the new tip. A diff or a folder is only of a (branch, commit) the run showed, approved or deployed: `main`, `HEAD`, `<tip>~1`, `--output=x.txt` and `..` are refused and no file is written. Tests: `workflows_test::a_check_shows_its_branches_at_their_tips_and_the_approval_records_what_it_showed`, `an_approval_of_tips_git_no_longer_has_is_refused_and_records_nothing`, `a_branch_s_diff_is_its_work_from_the_default_branch_and_other_refs_are_refused` (Task 3); `workflows_test::open_folder_finds_a_project_s_repo_and_a_ready_branch_s_worktree_by_id_only` (Task 6); `CheckPanel.test.tsx` `show the core's refusal when a branch moved, read the check again, and approve what it shows now` (Task 12).
3. **A lead that merged one member and left another, and a check that shows no branch.** Expected: the check shows and the deploy ships the lead's branch and the unmerged member's branch, not the merged one or a gone one; a `push`, `pr` or `pr_merge` after a check with no branch fails `Nothing to ship: the check approved no branch.` and never calls gh; `branch` mode succeeds. Tests: `workflows_test::a_check_shows_and_a_deploy_ships_the_member_branches_its_lead_did_not_merge`, `a_lead_that_merged_its_members_work_hands_on_its_own_branch_alone`, `a_deploy_after_a_check_that_showed_no_branch_fails_unless_it_only_lists_branches`; `deploy_test::push_pr_and_pr_merge_with_no_branch_fail_and_branch_mode_succeeds` (Task 2).
4. **Webhook calls that arrive at once.** Expected: of 5 calls at once, 3 get 202 and 2 get 429, and exactly 3 runs exist; a wrong token is still 401 and a workflow turned off still 409 at the cap; stopping one run lets one more start. Tests: `workflow_webhook_test::a_workflow_with_three_runs_in_flight_is_429_and_starts_nothing`, `calls_at_once_start_no_more_than_the_cap` (Task 1).
5. **The rail's Inbox count drifting from the core's.** Expected: a waiting workflow run is in `inbox().workflows` and `inbox_count`, and in the chat's dot's `needs_you`; once its chat run is deleted it stays in the Inbox and counts in no dot; it leaves when it goes on. The rail sums every Inbox list as `inbox_count` does, and a new `Inbox` field fails the test until the rail counts it. Tests: `runtime_seen_test::a_waiting_workflow_run_is_in_the_inbox_and_needs_the_dot_whose_chat_started_it`, `store_workflow_runs_test::a_waiting_workflow_run_needs_the_dot_whose_chat_started_it` (Task 5); `Layout.test.tsx` `counts every kind of Inbox item as the core's inbox count does`, `InboxWorkflows.test.tsx` `leaves the Inbox when the run goes on, read again on its event` (Task 14).
6. **A busy workflow flooding the person.** Expected: step runs never notify that they ended and are never Runs to review, while their approvals still notify and count; each wait of a workflow run is told once, a repeat is quiet, a second check right after the first is told, a cancelled run is quiet. Tests: `store_workflow_runs_test::a_step_s_runs_are_never_runs_to_review` (Task 5); `events::tests::a_step_s_runs_never_notify_that_they_ended_but_their_approvals_do`, `a_workflow_run_notifies_once_per_wait_and_when_it_ends`, `a_wait_on_a_new_step_run_is_a_new_notice` (Task 7).
7. **A new workflow, still off, being tried.** Expected: a Test run (manual, `test: true`) starts while the workflow is off and stays a test run; Run now and a `test` start by chat, schedule or webhook are still refused `workflow <name> is turned off`; the builder's Test run stays enabled while off. Tests: `workflows_test::a_test_run_starts_while_its_workflow_is_off_and_run_now_does_not` (Task 10); `WorkflowBuilderScreen.test.tsx` `saves the draft before a test run, then asks for its task, on or off` (Task 11).

---

## Context

Plans A and B left the core of teams and workflows complete: `teams.rs` and `store/teams.rs` keep teams, members, projects and workflows; `workflows::Workflows` moves the runs (agent, delegate, check, deploy and branch steps, send-backs, budget, retries, stop) and serves the workflow tools; the `Scheduler` and `POST /workflows/{id}/trigger` start them. Nothing in the app or the UI used them: `contract.rs` had no `Workflow`, the UI's `RuntimeEvent` lacked the three team events, and no command reached `approve_check` or `Workflows::stop`. Plans A and B's "Deviations from the spec" tables still hold, except where the table below says otherwise.

Tasks 1 and 2 build the person's decisions 1 to 4 in the core; Task 3 the check page's reads, the approval with tips and the branch diff; Task 4 the lists, the `Workflow` summary fields, live validation, the events and the webhook URL; Task 5 the Inbox, Needs you and Runs to review (decisions 5 and 6, core half); Task 6 the app commands, the contract and the UI's types; Task 7 the notices; Tasks 8, 9, 11 and 12 to 14 the screens (team page, builder, sheets, run page, chat card, Inbox); Task 10 a Test run while off; Task 15 a workflow run's dot runs; Task 16 the spec.

**How this plan's code was written:** every block below was generated from a prototype, one commit per task, on branch `proto/teams-c` (`.claude/worktrees/proto-teams-c`, based on `bac99662`): Task 1 `e7396bc1`, Task 2 `70e9bc0c`, Task 3 `379fa089`, Task 4 `6aec8749`, Task 5 `ebdc0e92`, Task 6 `21db3953`, Task 7 `5c83f6fd`, Task 8 `47eed1e4`, Task 9 `a7457935`, Task 10 `cf8cc99e`, Task 11 `953a1452`, Task 12 `c23b7f66`, Task 13 `c3961360`, Task 14 `2cc34338`, Task 15 `12eb58bf`, Task 16 `bcd0f469`. Each commit that touched Rust is `cargo fmt` clean, and at each one the prototype's `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets` gave no warning. At `bac99662` the prototype ran core 2122, app 176 and UI 1069 tests in 97 files, all passing; at Task 15, core 2149, app 180 and UI 1203 in 113. The blocks were generated by `.superpowers/tools/plan-gen/gen.py` (test files, by path, in Step 1; the others in Step 3; Cargo.lock and the contract fixture none) and then applied in order, by `replay.py`, which follows the headings above, to a fresh checkout of `bac99662`, comparing each task's tree with its commit.
The replay (`python replay.py <plan> <checkout> <proto> T1=… T16=…`, 2026-11-05, onto a clean detached checkout of `bac99662`) reported "matches" for all 16 tasks: after each task the staged tree equalled its prototype commit. `python apply_step.py <plan> <checkout> --check 1 bcd0f469 bac99662` found 0 problems in 421 blocks.
Step 2 of each task fails as it says: a Rust task fails to compile (a new type, field, constant or method the test uses does not exist yet) unless it says otherwise; a UI task's new test file that imports a module not written yet fails to load, and a test that renders the app fails at its first query for what is not there yet.

## Deviations from the spec

| Spec | This plan | Why |
|---|---|---|
| §7 "Sandboxed and Custom ask"; §17 "A Sandboxed step asks before `report_outcome`, `send_back` and `start_member`" | `Policy::resolve_in` turns an `Ask` into `Allow` when the tool is `Workflow` and `Tool::for_input` gives `ReportOutcome` or `SendBack`, under every preset, so a person's own `Workflow: ask` (or a default of ask) no longer makes these two ask. `start_workflow` and `start_member` still ask; a deny, by rule or default, refuses all four. Callers of `resolve(WORKFLOW_TOOL, &Null)` look only for `Deny` and are unchanged | Decision 1. The policy cannot tell the person's `ask` from the preset's: `Rule { tool, action }` carries no origin, `finish_0015` writes the same rule as `Policy::preset(Sandboxed)`, and Custom starts from the Sandboxed rules |
| (silent) the webhook's runs in flight | At most `MAX_WEBHOOK_IN_FLIGHT = 3` runs `running` or `waiting` per workflow, of any trigger; past it 429 `too many runs in flight`, checked after 401 and the 409s and before the body is read (so a bad body at the cap gets 429). One process-wide tokio mutex makes the count and the start one step and serialises webhook starts. Manual, chat and schedule starts are not capped. No `Retry-After` | Decision 3; without the lock, calls at once all pass the cap |
| §6.2 for a delegate step whose lead's branch has commits, "that branch alone" | The lead's branch first, then each member branch whose tip is not an ancestor of the lead's tip (`git merge-base --is-ancestor`), in the order the members started; a gone member branch is left out; when git cannot judge, the branch is shown. Applied once, when the hand-off is built (`handoff_of` through `delegate::handed_branches`), so the check, the approval and the deploy read the same list. With 2 or more branches the next agent step starts from the project's default branch with the branches in its prompt | Decision 2; showing too much is safer than hiding work |
| Plan B's row "§5.5 zero branches: succeeds"; §17's known gap | A `push`, `pr` or `pr_merge` deploy with no branch fails `Nothing to ship: the check approved no branch.` in `Deployer::deploy_into`, after the tip check and before the scan and `gh --version`; the default branch is not looked up; the run waits on `step_failed`. `branch` and `member` still succeed; a Test run deploys as `branch`, so it succeeds | Decision 4 |
| §5.4 Approve; §17 "plan C's approve call should send the tips the page showed" | `Workflows::check(id) -> CheckView` is read with no lock and changes nothing; `approve_check(id, note, tips)` re-reads the shown branches under the lock and refuses (`Conflict`, nothing recorded) a page that left out a branch, sent one twice, sent one the check does not show, or sent another commit; a gone branch passes only with `commit: null`. The recorded tips are git's, in the check's order | The approval is exactly what the person saw |
| §5.4 `show`: "the diff of each branch from the project's default branch" | `branch_diff(id, branch, commit)`: committed changes of `merge-base(<default>, <commit>)..<commit>`, capped as the worktree diff is. Allowed are the waiting check's branches at their tips now, every approved check's recorded tips and every deploy result's branches. An unknown branch is `Invalid`, a known branch at another commit `Conflict` (the changed-branches text), a missing default `Conflict(NO_DEFAULT_BRANCH)`. The default is the project's, else the remote's HEAD, else the repo's branch, as deploys find it, resolved as `refs/heads/<d>` then `refs/remotes/<remote>/<d>`. `check` and `branch_diff` need no permission | The run page shows diffs after the approval and on a deploy's result; nothing the page sends reaches git as a ref or an option |
| (silent) Stop's permission | `Workflows::stop` requires `RunDots` | As `add_budget` and `retry_step` |
| §5.8, §10.2 "validation errors show on the card they name" | `Teams::check_workflow(team_id, spec) -> Option<WorkflowProblem { step_id, message }>`: the name and the section 5.8 rules (`WorkflowSpec::problem`, which `validate` is now built on). It leaves out a name another workflow has and a schedule that would never run: Save still refuses both, shown on the builder's header. No permission | One source of rules; those two need the edited workflow's id, the stored schedule and the clock |
| §11 "workflow runs: list (by team, by status)" | `Workflows::list(&WorkflowRunFilter { team_id, workflow_id, statuses, limit })`, newest first; `for_origins(run_ids)` oldest first, ended runs included; `Workflow.runs_in_flight` and `Workflow.last_run` (`LastRun { id, status, started_at, test }`, the newest `started_at` of any status) | The builder and the team page read one type; the chat card shows the final state |
| §11 events | Also `TeamUpdated` for each team of a dot renamed or deleted, `WorkflowUpdated` for each workflow of a team whose set of member dot ids changes, and `WorkflowUpdated` for each workflow `Teams::delete` or `Workflows::delete_team` removes. No `WorkflowUpdated` when a run starts or ends: the UI refreshes its workflow queries on `workflow_run_updated` | A workflow's `problem` follows its members; one event per run move is enough |
| §10.5 "with no chat origin, it counts in the Inbox" | Every waiting run is in the Inbox's `workflows` and `inbox_count`; one with an `origin_run_id` also counts in that dot's `needs_you`; one whose origin run was deleted counts in the Inbox only. `WaitingWorkflowRun` has no `updated_at` (no such column) and no origin run id; its workflow name is `null` once the workflow is deleted; a row with `waiting_on` NULL is left out; newest first | Decision 5 |
| (silent) a step's dot runs | A dot run with `workflow_step_run_id` is left out of Runs to review and gets no "finished" or "failed" notice; its approvals, questions and `awaiting_approval` park notify as before. `unread`/`fresh` in `dot_counts` still counts it | Decision 6; "new since seen" is not Runs to review |
| §11 commands | 30 commands, one line each over the core. `open_project_folder(projectId, workflowRunId, branch)` takes exactly a project, or a workflow run and its branch, by id, through the new core `workflows/folder.rs` (`project_folder`, `branch_folder`: a branch a deploy result lists `ready`, at the newest hand-off's `worktree_path`). `workflow_run_start` always uses `manual` with no origin. `team_delete` calls `Workflows::delete_team` | The UI never sends a path; spec 5.1 has Run now and Test run both manual |
| §10.5 notifications | On `WorkflowRunUpdated` transitions only (status, wait, and the current step's newest step run): a check, the budget, the send-back limit (`<team> hit its send-back limit`, body `send-backs, <n> of <max>`), finished, and a wait on a failed step told as `<team> failed` with the run's error. ` (test)` on every title of a Test run. A run first seen waiting or ended notifies. No notice for a cancelled run, a repeat, or a `failed` after a step-failed wait. The viewing rule applies through the origin chat's dot. No setting turns them off (the only one is `notify_on_notes`) | The core never sets `failed`: a failed step waits; a second check right after a first must be told; a first sighting is a move made while the app was not listening |
| §5.1 a Test run | A Test run (`test: true` with the `manual` trigger) starts while the workflow is off; every other start of an off workflow is refused `workflow <name> is turned off`; a problem still refuses a Test run | The controller's call (2026-11-04, after Task 9): `enabled` arms the chat, schedule and webhook triggers and Run now, so a new workflow can be tried before it is armed |
| §10.1 the team page | The teams list shows every team; a new team is made in the scope the person works in; the member picker offers the team's account and workspace only; a role saves when its field is left. Each workflow row also has Run now (disabled with a reason) and a switch that saves at once. New workflow has no dialog: `New workflow` (unique), turned off, one agent step for the first member, the default budget. Delete team says runs in flight are stopped. A **Waiting** list (from `inbox.workflows`, by team) comes first, above Members | Spec 10.5: "listed on the team page"; a half-built workflow cannot start |

Cards move up and down within their own list only (buttons, Alt+ArrowUp/Down), with no drag handle. Deleting a step is at once, except a branch that holds steps. Outcomes are added and removed, not renamed. A workflow's schedule offers no None and no catch-up. Test run saves first. Delete workflow is in the builder. Nothing guards leaving an unsaved draft. A PR link is followed only when it is `https://` | `workflow_add_budget(id)` takes no amounts; the first version's newest-100 read missed runs |

### Task 1: `report_outcome` and `send_back` no longer ask on Sandboxed dots, and the webhook caps the runs in flight

Decisions 1 and 3. `Policy::resolve_in` turns an `Ask` for `Workflow` into `Allow` when the call is `report_outcome` or `send_back` (told apart by `Tool::for_input`; both engines keep only the called tool's own arguments before the gate, so a `start_*` call cannot carry `outcome` or `to_step`). The webhook counts the workflow's runs in flight after the token and the 409s, under one lock held until the start returns, and answers 429 at 3.

**Files:**
- Modify: `crates/dots-core/src/{policy.rs,server/workflow_webhook.rs,store/workflow_runs.rs}`
- Test: `crates/dots-core/tests/{workflow_tools_test,local_tools_test,workflow_webhook_test}.rs`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: plan B's `Policy::resolve_in`, `team_tools::Tool::for_input`, the webhook handler `server::workflow_webhook::trigger`, `Workflows::start`.
- Produces: private `policy::never_asks(tool: &str, input: &Value) -> bool`; `Store::count_workflow_runs_in_flight(&self, workflow_id: &str) -> Result<u32>` (runs `running` or `waiting`); `server::workflow_webhook::{MAX_WEBHOOK_IN_FLIGHT: u32 = 3, TOO_MANY_IN_FLIGHT: &str = "too many runs in flight"}` and a private `static STARTS: tokio::sync::Mutex<()>`; the answer 429 `{"error": "too many runs in flight"}`. Test helper `Rt::started_and_stopped(&self, answer: (u16, Value)) -> WorkflowRun`.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

`a_sandboxed_step_asks_before_it_reports_an_outcome` is replaced by `a_sandboxed_step_reports_its_outcome_and_sends_back_without_asking`. `the_body_falls_back_to_the_trigger_s_task_and_project` starts 7 runs of one workflow, so it now stops each after its 202 (`started_and_stopped`); its assertions are unchanged.

```rust
/// Under Sandboxed a step's `report_outcome` and `send_back` pass the gate without asking, and
/// `start_workflow` and `start_member` still ask, even with a decoy `outcome` or `to_step`
/// (dropped before the gate, so a call cannot pass for one of the two). A `Workflow: deny`
/// refuses all four.
#[test]
fn the_gate_asks_for_start_workflow_and_start_member_only() {
    let ws = workspace();
    let p = ws.path();
    let sandboxed = Policy::preset(Preset::Sandboxed);
    let mut denies = sandboxed.clone();
    denies.rules.push(Rule::new("Workflow", Action::Deny));
    let decoys = json!({"outcome": "fixed", "to_step": "Build", "note": "x"});
    let calls = [
        (
            "start_workflow",
            json!({"workflow": "Fix", "project": "Dots", "task": "Fix it."}),
            Action::Ask,
        ),
        (
            "start_member",
            json!({"member": "dev", "task": "Fix it."}),
            Action::Ask,
        ),
        ("report_outcome", json!({"outcome": "fixed"}), Action::Allow),
        (
            "send_back",
            json!({"to_step": "Build", "note": "Use the light icon."}),
            Action::Allow,
        ),
    ];
    for (name, args, want) in calls {
        let (input, action) = gate(p, &sandboxed, name, args.clone());
        assert_eq!(input, args, "{name}");
        assert_eq!(action, want, "{name}");
        let mut decoyed = args.clone();
        for (key, value) in decoys.as_object().unwrap() {
            decoyed
                .as_object_mut()
                .unwrap()
                .entry(key.clone())
                .or_insert(value.clone());
        }
        let (input, action) = gate(p, &sandboxed, name, decoyed);
        assert_eq!(input, args, "{name} with decoys");
        assert_eq!(action, want, "{name} with decoys");
        assert_eq!(gate(p, &denies, name, args).1, Action::Deny, "{name}");
    }
}
```

```rust
/// The `Workflow` input of each workflow tool, as `team_tools::Tool::for_input` reads it.
fn workflow_calls() -> [(&'static str, Value); 4] {
    [
        (
            "start_workflow",
            json!({"workflow": "Fix", "project": "Dots", "task": "Fix it."}),
        ),
        ("start_member", json!({"member": "dev", "task": "Fix it."})),
        ("report_outcome", json!({"outcome": "fixed"})),
        (
            "send_back",
            json!({"to_step": "Build", "note": "Use the light icon."}),
        ),
    ]
}

/// `report_outcome` and `send_back` never ask (the person's decision, 2026-11-04): under
/// Sandboxed and Custom, under the person's own `Workflow: ask` and under a default of ask,
/// they are allowed, while `start_workflow` and `start_member` still ask. A `Workflow: deny` or
/// a default of deny refuses all four.
#[test]
fn report_outcome_and_send_back_never_ask_and_a_deny_refuses_every_workflow_tool() {
    let asking = |name: &str| matches!(name, "start_workflow" | "start_member");
    for preset in [Preset::Sandboxed, Preset::Custom] {
        let p = Policy::preset(preset);
        for (name, input) in workflow_calls() {
            let want = if asking(name) {
                Action::Ask
            } else {
                Action::Allow
            };
            assert_eq!(p.resolve(WORKFLOW_TOOL, &input), want, "{preset:?} {name}");
        }
    }
    let mut own = Policy::preset(Preset::Trusted);
    own.rules.push(Rule::new("Workflow", Action::Ask));
    let mut no_rule = Policy::preset(Preset::Custom);
    no_rule.rules.retain(|r| r.tool != "Workflow");
    assert_eq!(no_rule.default, Action::Ask);
    for p in [&own, &no_rule] {
        for (name, input) in workflow_calls() {
            let want = if asking(name) {
                Action::Ask
            } else {
                Action::Allow
            };
            assert_eq!(p.resolve(WORKFLOW_TOOL, &input), want, "{name}");
        }
    }
    let mut denies = Policy::preset(Preset::Sandboxed);
    denies.rules.push(Rule::new("Workflow", Action::Deny));
    let mut by_default = no_rule.clone();
    by_default.default = Action::Deny;
    for p in [&denies, &by_default] {
        for (name, input) in workflow_calls() {
            assert_eq!(p.resolve(WORKFLOW_TOOL, &input), Action::Deny, "{name}");
        }
    }
    // Only `Workflow`: another tool's input with an outcome still asks as before.
    let sandboxed = Policy::preset(Preset::Sandboxed);
    assert_eq!(
        sandboxed.resolve("WebFetch", &json!({"outcome": "fixed"})),
        Action::Ask
    );
}
```

```rust
    /// `started`, then the run is stopped, so it is not counted in flight
    /// (`MAX_WEBHOOK_IN_FLIGHT`).
    async fn started_and_stopped(&self, answer: (u16, Value)) -> WorkflowRun {
```

```rust
/// The workflow's runs that have not ended, by the store.
async fn in_flight(t: &Rt, workflow_id: &str) -> u32 {
    t.rt.store()
        .count_workflow_runs_in_flight(workflow_id)
        .await
        .unwrap()
}

/// Past `MAX_WEBHOOK_IN_FLIGHT` (3) runs in flight, waiting on the person's check included, the
/// webhook answers 429 and starts nothing, whatever the body; the token and the 409s are
/// answered first. Another workflow of the team is not held back, and a run that ends makes
/// room again.
#[tokio::test]
async fn a_workflow_with_three_runs_in_flight_is_429_and_starts_nothing() {
    use dots_core::model::WorkflowRunStatus;
    use dots_core::server::workflow_webhook::{MAX_WEBHOOK_IN_FLIGHT, TOO_MANY_IN_FLIGHT};
    assert_eq!(MAX_WEBHOOK_IN_FLIGHT, 3);
    assert_eq!(TOO_MANY_IN_FLIGHT, "too many runs in flight");
    let t = Rt::start().await;
    let (workflow, token) = t.hooked(Some(TRIGGER_TASK), Some("Dots")).await;
    let mut ids = Vec::new();
    for _ in 0..3 {
        ids.push(
            t.started(t.post(&workflow.id, Some(&token), None).await)
                .await
                .id,
        );
    }
    // Each waits on the check: a waiting run is in flight.
    let deadline = std::time::Instant::now() + std::time::Duration::from_secs(30);
    for id in &ids {
        while t.rt.workflows().get(id).await.unwrap().status != WorkflowRunStatus::Waiting {
            assert!(std::time::Instant::now() < deadline, "{id} never waited");
            tokio::time::sleep(std::time::Duration::from_millis(30)).await;
        }
    }
    let too_many = (429, json!({"error": "too many runs in flight"}));
    assert_eq!(t.post(&workflow.id, Some(&token), None).await, too_many);
    // Before the body is read.
    assert_eq!(
        t.post(&workflow.id, Some(&token), Some("not json")).await,
        too_many
    );
    // The token first, then the 409s.
    assert_eq!(t.post(&workflow.id, Some("wrong"), None).await.0, 401);
    let mut off = flow(webhook(Some(TRIGGER_TASK), Some("Dots")), check());
    off.enabled = false;
    t.rt.teams()
        .update_workflow(&workflow.id, &off)
        .await
        .unwrap();
    assert_eq!(
        t.post(&workflow.id, Some(&token), None).await,
        (409, json!({"error": "workflow Fix is turned off"}))
    );
    t.rt.teams()
        .update_workflow(
            &workflow.id,
            &flow(webhook(Some(TRIGGER_TASK), Some("Dots")), check()),
        )
        .await
        .unwrap();
    assert_eq!(t.post(&workflow.id, Some(&token), None).await, too_many);
    assert_eq!(t.runs().await, 3);
    assert_eq!(in_flight(&t, &workflow.id).await, 3);
    // Another workflow of the team starts.
    let mut ship = flow(webhook(Some(TRIGGER_TASK), Some("Dots")), check());
    ship.name = "Ship".into();
    let other = t.rt.teams().create_workflow(&t.team, &ship).await.unwrap();
    let other_token =
        t.rt.teams()
            .regenerate_workflow_token(&other.id)
            .await
            .unwrap();
    t.started(t.post(&other.id, Some(&other_token), None).await)
        .await;
    // A stopped run makes room for one more.
    t.rt.workflows().stop(&ids[0]).await.unwrap();
    t.started(t.post(&workflow.id, Some(&token), None).await)
        .await;
    assert_eq!(t.post(&workflow.id, Some(&token), None).await, too_many);
    assert_eq!(in_flight(&t, &workflow.id).await, 3);
    t.rt.shutdown();
}

/// Calls at once cannot pass the cap together: of five, three start and two are 429.
#[tokio::test]
async fn calls_at_once_start_no_more_than_the_cap() {
    let t = Rt::start().await;
    let (workflow, token) = t.hooked(Some(TRIGGER_TASK), Some("Dots")).await;
    let calls = (0..5).map(|_| t.post(&workflow.id, Some(&token), None));
    let mut codes: Vec<u16> = futures_util::future::join_all(calls)
        .await
        .into_iter()
        .map(|(status, _)| status)
        .collect();
    codes.sort_unstable();
    assert_eq!(codes, [202, 202, 202, 429, 429]);
    assert_eq!(t.runs().await, 3);
    assert_eq!(in_flight(&t, &workflow.id).await, 3);
    t.rt.shutdown();
}
```

```rust
    /// How many runs of the workflow have not ended (running or waiting).
    pub async fn count_workflow_runs_in_flight(&self, workflow_id: &str) -> Result<u32> {
        let n: i64 = sqlx::query_scalar(
            "SELECT COUNT(*) FROM workflow_runs WHERE workflow_id = ? \
             AND status IN ('running', 'waiting')",
        )
        .bind(workflow_id)
        .fetch_one(&self.pool)
        .await?;
        Ok(u32::try_from(n).unwrap_or(u32::MAX))
    }
```

### Task 2: A check shows the member branches its lead did not merge, and a deploy with no branch fails

Decisions 2 and 4. `handoff_of` collects the lead's branch (it exists and, for a delegate, has commits) and hands it to `delegate::handed_branches`, which keeps the member branches whose tip is not an ancestor of the lead's. The rule runs when the hand-off is built, in its job outside the loop's lock, so the check, the approval and the deploy agree. `Deployer::deploy_into` fails a `push`, `pr` or `pr_merge` with no branch before the scan and gh; `deploy_now` looks up the default branch only when there is something to ship.

**Files:**
- Modify: `crates/dots-core/src/{deploy.rs,workflows.rs,workflows/delegate.rs,workflows/deploy_step.rs}`
- Test: `crates/dots-core/tests/{workflows_test,deploy_test}.rs`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: plan B's `handoff_of`, `delegate::member_end`, `has_commits`, `BranchRef { run_id, branch, worktree_path, base_commit }`, `Deployer::deploy_into`, `check_tips`, `deploy_step::deploy_now`.
- Produces: private `workflows::merged_into(repo: &Path, branch: &str, of: &str) -> bool` (`git merge-base --is-ancestor --end-of-options refs/heads/<branch> refs/heads/<of>`, `true` only on exit 0); `delegate::handed_branches(repo: &Path, lead: Option<BranchRef>, members: Vec<BranchRef>) -> Vec<BranchRef>` (`pub(super)`, async); `deploy::NOTHING_TO_SHIP = "Nothing to ship: the check approved no branch."`. Test helpers in `workflows_test.rs`: `Act::MergeFirst(file, summary)` (merges the first existing member branch of the run's step run, writes `file`, finishes) and `branch_in(dir: &Path, branch: &str) -> bool`.

```rust
#[tokio::test]
async fn push_pr_and_pr_merge_with_no_branch_fail_and_branch_mode_succeeds() {
    let s = scratch();
    let modes = [
        DeployMode::Push,
        DeployMode::Pr,
        DeployMode::PrMerge {
            merge_method: MergeMethod::Squash,
        },
    ];
    for mode in modes {
        let (err, done) = s.deploy_keeping("ok", &mode, &[]).await.unwrap_err();
        assert_eq!(err, NOTHING_TO_SHIP);
        assert_eq!(err, "Nothing to ship: the check approved no branch.");
        assert!(done.branches.is_empty());
        assert!(!done.ready);
        assert_eq!(done.error, None);
    }
    assert!(s.gh_calls().is_empty(), "gh is not even asked its version");
    let result = s.deploy("ok", &DeployMode::Branch, &[]).await.unwrap();
    assert!(result.ready);
    assert!(result.branches.is_empty());
}
```

```rust
/// Whether the branch `branch` exists in the repository of `dir`.
fn branch_in(dir: &Path, branch: &str) -> bool {
    std::process::Command::new("git")
        .arg("-C")
        .arg(dir)
        .args(["rev-parse", "--verify", "--quiet"])
        .arg(format!("refs/heads/{branch}"))
        .output()
        .unwrap()
        .status
        .success()
}
```

```rust
#[tokio::test]
async fn a_check_shows_and_a_deploy_ships_the_member_branches_its_lead_did_not_merge() {
    let w = world().await;
    w.shipping_to_origin("ok");
    w.engine.set(
        &w.helper.spec.name,
        vec![
            Act::Start(
                vec![
                    ("dev", "Part one."),
                    ("dev", "Part two."),
                    ("dev", "Part three."),
                ],
                Box::new(Act::Finish("Started three.")),
            ),
            Act::MergeFirst("merged.txt", "Merged one part."),
        ],
    );
    // Two members change something; the third changes nothing, so its branch goes with its
    // worktree.
    w.engine.set(
        &w.dev.spec.name,
        vec![
            Act::Write("one.txt", "One done."),
            Act::Write("two.txt", "Two done."),
            Act::Finish("Nothing to change."),
        ],
    );
    let flow = w
        .workflow(
            "Split",
            vec![
                delegating("split", "Split it", &w.helper, &[&w.dev], 3, 6),
                check("check"),
                ship(DeployMode::Push),
            ],
        )
        .await;
    let wr = w.start(&flow).await;
    let wr = w.until(&wr.id, waits_on(WaitingOn::Check)).await;
    let split = newest(&wr, "split").clone();
    let members = w.members_are(&split.id, 3, RunStatus::Succeeded).await;
    let lead = w.lead_is(&split.id, RunStatus::Succeeded).await;
    let own = lead.branch.clone().unwrap();
    let kept: Vec<String> = members
        .iter()
        .filter_map(|m| m.branch.clone())
        .filter(|b| branch_in(&w.repo, b))
        .collect();
    assert_eq!(kept.len(), 2, "{members:#?}");
    let (merged, other) = (&kept[0], &kept[1]);
    // The lead merged the first part: its branch has commits and the first member's tip is
    // in it, but not the second's.
    git_out(&w.repo, &["merge-base", "--is-ancestor", merged, &own]);
    assert_ne!(
        git_out(&w.repo, &["rev-parse", merged]),
        git_out(&w.repo, &["rev-parse", &own])
    );
    // The check shows the lead's branch, then the member branch it did not merge; the
    // merged one and the one that is gone are left out.
    let handoff = split.handoff.clone().unwrap();
    let shown: Vec<&str> = handoff.branches.iter().map(|b| b.branch.as_str()).collect();
    assert_eq!(shown, [own.as_str(), other.as_str()]);
    // The lead's branch is named by the newest run of its thread: the one that merged.
    let named = w.store.get_run(&handoff.branches[0].run_id).await.unwrap();
    assert_eq!(named.root_run_id, lead.id);
    assert_eq!(handoff.members.len(), 3);
    assert_eq!(
        handoff
            .members
            .iter()
            .filter(|m| m.branch.is_none())
            .count(),
        1
    );
    // The approval records the tips of exactly those branches, and the deploy pushes them.
    let tip = |b: &str| git_out(&w.repo, &["rev-parse", &format!("refs/heads/{b}")]);
    let (own_tip, other_tip) = (tip(&own), tip(other));
    let approved = w.workflows.approve_check(&wr.id, None).await.unwrap();
    assert_eq!(
        newest(&approved, "check").decision.as_ref().unwrap()["branches"],
        json!([
            {"branch": own, "commit": own_tip},
            {"branch": other, "commit": other_tip},
        ])
    );
    let wr = w
        .until(&wr.id, |wr| wr.status == WorkflowRunStatus::Succeeded)
        .await;
    let result = newest(&wr, "ship").deploy_result.clone().unwrap();
    assert_eq!(
        result.branches,
        [pushed(&own, &own_tip), pushed(other, &other_tip)]
    );
    assert_eq!(w.remote_tip(&own), Some(own_tip));
    assert_eq!(w.remote_tip(other), Some(other_tip));
    assert_eq!(w.remote_tip(merged), None);
}
```

```rust
/// Whether the tip of the branch `branch` is an ancestor of the tip of the branch `of`, or the
/// same commit: all the work of `branch` is in `of`. `false` when git cannot tell (a branch
/// gone), so the branch is shown rather than hidden.
async fn merged_into(repo: &Path, branch: &str, of: &str) -> bool {
    let branch = format!("refs/heads/{branch}");
    let of = format!("refs/heads/{of}");
    let args = [
        "merge-base",
        "--is-ancestor",
        "--end-of-options",
        &branch,
        &of,
    ];
    git(repo, &args).await.is_ok()
}
```

```rust
/// The branches a step hands on, so what a check after it shows and a deploy after it ships
/// (section 6.2, with the person's ancestor rule of 2026-11-04). `lead` is the step's own
/// branch, for a delegate step only when it has commits; `members` are the branches of the
/// members it started that still exist, in the order started. With a lead branch: that branch,
/// then each member branch whose tip is not an ancestor of the lead's tip (work the lead did not
/// merge). Without one: every member branch. A step with no members hands on its own branch.
pub(super) async fn handed_branches(
    repo: &Path,
    lead: Option<BranchRef>,
    members: Vec<BranchRef>,
) -> Vec<BranchRef> {
    let Some(lead) = lead else {
        return members;
    };
    let mut out = vec![lead];
    for member in members {
        if !merged_into(repo, &member.branch, &out[0].branch).await {
            out.push(member);
        }
    }
    out
}

```

### Task 3: A check is read before it is approved, approval names the tips it saw, and a branch's diff is from the default branch

`workflows/check.rs` holds what the check page reads: `Workflows::check` gives the waiting check's summary and branches with their tips (`git rev-parse`, outside the lock); `approve_check` takes the tips the page showed and refuses, recording nothing, when they differ from git's under the lock; `branch_diff` diffs a branch the run showed, approved or deployed from its merge base with the project's default branch (`workspace::range_diff`). `Workflows::stop` now requires `RunDots`.

**Files:**
- Create: `crates/dots-core/src/workflows/check.rs`
- Modify: `crates/dots-core/src/{workflows.rs,workflows/deploy_step.rs,workspace.rs}`
- Test: `crates/dots-core/tests/{workflows_test,runtime_roles_test}.rs`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: Task 2's hand-off (`shown_at` already applies the ancestor rule); plan B's `deploy_step::{tips, base_branch}`, `NO_DEFAULT_BRANCH`, `workspace::{parse_diff, MAX_DIFF_BYTES, WorktreeDiff}`.
- Produces: `workflows::{CheckView { step_id: String, summary: String, branches: Vec<ShownBranch> }, ShownBranch { branch: String, run_id: String, commit: Option<String> }, CheckTip { branch: String, commit: Option<String> }, CheckView::tips(&self) -> Vec<CheckTip>, branches_changed(names: &[String]) -> String}` (re-exported from `workflows/check.rs`; JSON snake_case, a `None` commit is `null`); `Workflows::{check(&self, id: &str) -> Result<CheckView>, approve_check(&self, id: &str, note: Option<&str>, tips: &[CheckTip]) -> Result<WorkflowRun>, branch_diff(&self, id: &str, branch: &str, commit: &str) -> Result<WorktreeDiff>}`; `Workflows::stop` unchanged in signature, now `RunDots`; `workspace::range_diff(repo: &Path, from: &str, to: &str) -> Result<WorktreeDiff>` and a private `cap(&mut [FileDiff]) -> bool` that `diff` uses too; `deploy_step::tip(repo, branch) -> Option<String>` (`pub(super)`), `tips()` now `Vec<CheckTip>` (same JSON), `base_branch` now `pub(super)`. Test helpers `World::approve(id, note)` (reads `check` and approves its `tips()`, as the page does), `World::commit_file(branch, path, text) -> String`, `conflict(result) -> String`, `changed(branch) -> String`.

```rust
impl World {
    /// Moves `branch` on by a commit that adds the file `path` with `text`, made outside any
    /// worktree (in an index of its own), as someone working on it would. Gives the new tip.
    fn commit_file(&self, branch: &str, path: &str, text: &str) -> String {
        let tip = git_out(&self.repo, &["rev-parse", &format!("refs/heads/{branch}")]);
        let file = self.dir.path().join("blob.txt");
        std::fs::write(&file, text).unwrap();
        let blob = git_out(&self.repo, &["hash-object", "-w", &file.to_string_lossy()]);
        let index = self.dir.path().join("index.tmp");
        let git = |args: &[&str]| {
            let out = std::process::Command::new("git")
                .arg("-C")
                .arg(&self.repo)
                .env("GIT_INDEX_FILE", &index)
                .args(args)
                .output()
                .unwrap();
            assert!(out.status.success(), "git {args:?}");
            String::from_utf8_lossy(&out.stdout).trim().to_string()
        };
        git(&["read-tree", &tip]);
        let entry = format!("100644,{blob},{path}");
        git(&["update-index", "--add", "--cacheinfo", &entry]);
        let tree = git(&["write-tree"]);
        let later = git_out(
            &self.repo,
            &["commit-tree", &tree, "-p", &tip, "-m", "More."],
        );
        git_out(
            &self.repo,
            &["update-ref", &format!("refs/heads/{branch}"), &later],
        );
        std::fs::remove_file(&index).unwrap();
        later
    }
}

/// The text of a `Conflict` refusal; anything else fails the test.
fn conflict<T: std::fmt::Debug>(result: dots_core::Result<T>) -> String {
    match result {
        Err(Error::Conflict(text)) => text,
        other => panic!("expected Conflict, got {other:?}"),
    }
}

/// Why an approval or a diff of `branch` is refused when the page saw another tip.
fn changed(branch: &str) -> String {
    format!("The branches changed since the check was shown: {branch}. Look at the check again.")
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn a_check_shows_its_branches_at_their_tips_and_the_approval_records_what_it_showed() {
    let w = world().await;
    let flow = w.ship_flow(DeployMode::Branch).await;
    let (wr, branch, tip) = w.at_the_check(&flow, false).await;
    let dev = newest(&wr, "dev").handoff.clone().unwrap();
    let run_id = dev.branches[0].run_id.clone();
    let view = w.workflows.check(&wr.id).await.unwrap();
    assert_eq!(
        view,
        CheckView {
            step_id: "check".into(),
            summary: "Fixed the icon.".into(),
            branches: vec![ShownBranch {
                branch: branch.clone(),
                run_id: run_id.clone(),
                commit: Some(tip.clone()),
            }],
        }
    );
    // What the app is given.
    assert_eq!(
        serde_json::to_value(&view).unwrap(),
        json!({
            "step_id": "check",
            "summary": "Fixed the icon.",
            "branches": [{"branch": branch, "run_id": run_id, "commit": tip}],
        })
    );
    assert_eq!(
        view.tips(),
        vec![CheckTip {
            branch: branch.clone(),
            commit: Some(tip.clone()),
        }]
    );
    // Reading it changes nothing.
    let still = w.store.get_workflow_run(&wr.id).await.unwrap();
    assert_eq!(still.waiting_on, Some(WaitingOn::Check));
    assert_eq!(newest(&still, "check").decision, None);

    w.workflows
        .approve_check(&wr.id, Some("Ship it."), &view.tips())
        .await
        .unwrap();
    let wr = w
        .until(&wr.id, |wr| wr.status == WorkflowRunStatus::Succeeded)
        .await;
    assert_eq!(
        newest(&wr, "check").decision,
        Some(json!({
            "decision": "approved",
            "note": "Ship it.",
            "branches": [{"branch": branch, "commit": tip}],
        }))
    );
    assert_eq!(
        conflict(w.workflows.check(&wr.id).await),
        format!("workflow run {} is not waiting for a check", wr.id)
    );
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn an_approval_of_tips_git_no_longer_has_is_refused_and_records_nothing() {
    let w = world().await;
    let flow = w.ship_flow(DeployMode::Branch).await;
    let (wr, branch, _) = w.at_the_check(&flow, false).await;
    let seen = w.workflows.check(&wr.id).await.unwrap().tips();
    // The branch moves on after the page read the check.
    let later = w.commit_after(&branch);
    let approve = |tips: Vec<CheckTip>| {
        let w = &w;
        let id = wr.id.clone();
        async move {
            w.workflows
                .approve_check(&id, Some("Ship it."), &tips)
                .await
        }
    };
    assert_eq!(conflict(approve(seen).await), changed(&branch));
    let fresh = w.workflows.check(&wr.id).await.unwrap().tips();
    assert_eq!(
        fresh,
        vec![CheckTip {
            branch: branch.clone(),
            commit: Some(later.clone()),
        }]
    );
    // A page that showed no branch, one the check does not show, or the same one twice.
    assert_eq!(conflict(approve(vec![]).await), changed(&branch));
    let mut more = fresh.clone();
    more.push(CheckTip {
        branch: "main".into(),
        commit: None,
    });
    assert_eq!(conflict(approve(more).await), changed("main"));
    let twice = vec![fresh[0].clone(), fresh[0].clone()];
    assert_eq!(conflict(approve(twice).await), changed(&branch));
    // Nothing was recorded.
    let still = w.store.get_workflow_run(&wr.id).await.unwrap();
    assert_eq!(still.status, WorkflowRunStatus::Waiting);
    assert_eq!(still.waiting_on, Some(WaitingOn::Check));
    assert_eq!(newest(&still, "check").status, StepRunStatus::Waiting);
    assert_eq!(newest(&still, "check").decision, None);
    assert!(still.steps.iter().all(|s| s.step_id != "ship"));

    // Read again, the approval records the tip the page now showed, and the deploy lists it.
    approve(fresh).await.unwrap();
    let wr = w
        .until(&wr.id, |wr| wr.status == WorkflowRunStatus::Succeeded)
        .await;
    assert_eq!(
        newest(&wr, "check").decision.as_ref().unwrap()["branches"],
        json!([{"branch": branch, "commit": later}])
    );
    let listed = newest(&wr, "ship").deploy_result.clone().unwrap();
    assert_eq!(listed.branches[0].commit, later);
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn a_branch_s_diff_is_its_work_from_the_default_branch_and_other_refs_are_refused() {
    let w = world().await;
    let flow = w.ship_flow(DeployMode::Branch).await;
    let (wr, branch, tip) = w.at_the_check(&flow, false).await;
    // The default branch moves on meanwhile: its new work is not the branch's.
    std::fs::write(w.repo.join("later.txt"), "On main.\n").unwrap();
    git_out(&w.repo, &["add", "later.txt"]);
    git_out(&w.repo, &["commit", "-q", "-m", "Later on main."]);
    let main = git_out(&w.repo, &["rev-parse", "refs/heads/main"]);

    let diff = w
        .workflows
        .branch_diff(&wr.id, &branch, &tip)
        .await
        .unwrap();
    let files: Vec<_> = diff
        .files
        .iter()
        .map(|f| (f.path.as_str(), f.status))
        .collect();
    assert_eq!(files, [("fix.txt", FileStatus::Added)]);
    assert!(
        diff.files[0].patch.contains("+Fixed the icon."),
        "{}",
        diff.files[0].patch
    );
    assert!(!diff.truncated);
    assert!(diff.exists);

    // Only a branch the run shows: not the default branch, a ref, or an option.
    for name in ["main", "HEAD", "--output=x.txt"] {
        match w.workflows.branch_diff(&wr.id, name, &tip).await {
            Err(Error::Invalid(m)) => {
                assert_eq!(m, format!("workflow run {} shows no branch {name}", wr.id))
            }
            other => panic!("expected Invalid, got {other:?}"),
        }
    }
    // And only at the commit it shows: not a ref, another commit, or an option.
    let before = format!("{tip}~1");
    for commit in [
        "HEAD",
        "main",
        main.as_str(),
        before.as_str(),
        "--output=x.txt",
    ] {
        assert_eq!(
            conflict(w.workflows.branch_diff(&wr.id, &branch, commit).await),
            changed(&branch)
        );
    }
    assert!(!w.repo.join("x.txt").exists());

    // Past the cap, the later files are listed without their patch.
    let big = "x".repeat(dots_core::workspace::MAX_DIFF_BYTES) + "\n";
    let moved = w.commit_file(&branch, "big.txt", &big);
    let diff = w
        .workflows
        .branch_diff(&wr.id, &branch, &moved)
        .await
        .unwrap();
    let files: Vec<_> = diff
        .files
        .iter()
        .map(|f| (f.path.as_str(), f.status, f.patch.is_empty()))
        .collect();
    assert_eq!(
        files,
        [
            ("big.txt", FileStatus::Added, true),
            ("fix.txt", FileStatus::Added, false),
        ]
    );
    assert!(diff.truncated);
    // The check now shows the new tip, so the old one is refused.
    assert_eq!(
        conflict(w.workflows.branch_diff(&wr.id, &branch, &tip).await),
        changed(&branch)
    );

    // Once approved and listed by the deploy, the branch is diffed at the commit recorded.
    w.approve(&wr.id, None).await.unwrap();
    let wr = w
        .until(&wr.id, |wr| wr.status == WorkflowRunStatus::Succeeded)
        .await;
    let diff = w
        .workflows
        .branch_diff(&wr.id, &branch, &moved)
        .await
        .unwrap();
    assert_eq!(diff.files.len(), 2);
    assert_eq!(
        conflict(w.workflows.branch_diff(&wr.id, &branch, &tip).await),
        changed(&branch)
    );
    // With no default branch to diff from, it says so.
    git_out(&w.repo, &["checkout", "-q", "--detach"]);
    assert_eq!(
        conflict(w.workflows.branch_diff(&wr.id, &branch, &moved).await),
        NO_DEFAULT_BRANCH
    );
}
```

**Create `crates/dots-core/src/workflows/check.rs`:**

```rust
//! What a waiting check shows the person (teams spec sections 5.4 and 6.2), and the diff of a
//! branch it shows. The check page reads `check` before the person decides: the branches of the
//! hand-off the check was shown on (`shown_at`, which already holds the ancestor rule), each
//! with the commit at its tip. Approve sends those tips back (`CheckTip`), and the approval is
//! refused when git no longer agrees with them (`changed`), so the person never approves work
//! they did not see. `branch_diff` shows a branch's committed work from the project's default
//! branch, for a branch the run's check shows or its deploy names only: the page never names
//! an arbitrary ref.

use std::path::Path;

use serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};
use serde_json::Value;

use super::deploy_step::{base_branch, tip};
use super::{latest, shown_at, Workflows, NO_DEFAULT_BRANCH};
use crate::model::{StepRun, WaitingOn, WorkflowRun, WorkflowRunStatus, WorktreeDiff};
use crate::workspace::{git, range_diff};
use crate::{Error, Result};

/// A branch a waiting check shows: the run whose branch it is, and the commit at its tip now
/// (`None` for a branch that is gone).
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct ShownBranch {
    pub branch: String,
    pub run_id: String,
    pub commit: Option<String>,
}

/// What the check a workflow run waits on shows (section 5.4): the check step, the summary of
/// the hand-off it was shown on, and that hand-off's branches.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct CheckView {
    pub step_id: String,
    pub summary: String,
    pub branches: Vec<ShownBranch>,
}

/// A branch as the check page showed it, with the commit it showed (`None` for a branch that
/// was gone): what an approval names, and what it records (section 6.2).
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct CheckTip {
    pub branch: String,
    pub commit: Option<String>,
}

impl CheckView {
    /// The tips this view shows, as an approval of it names them.
    pub fn tips(&self) -> Vec<CheckTip> {
        self.branches
            .iter()
            .map(|b| CheckTip {
                branch: b.branch.clone(),
                commit: b.commit.clone(),
            })
            .collect()
    }
}

/// Why an approval is refused when what the page showed is not what git has now: `names` are
/// the branches that differ.
pub fn branches_changed(names: &[String]) -> String {
    format!(
        "The branches changed since the check was shown: {}. Look at the check again.",
        names.join(", ")
    )
}

/// The branches whose tips differ between `now` (git's, in the order the check shows them) and
/// `seen` (the page's): a branch shown with another commit or not sent at all, then a branch
/// sent that the check does not show or sent twice. Empty when the page saw exactly `now`.
pub(super) fn changed(now: &[CheckTip], seen: &[CheckTip]) -> Vec<String> {
    let mut out: Vec<String> = Vec::new();
    for tip in now {
        let same = seen.iter().find(|s| s.branch == tip.branch);
        if same.is_none_or(|s| s.commit != tip.commit) {
            out.push(tip.branch.clone());
        }
    }
    for s in seen {
        let shown = now.iter().any(|t| t.branch == s.branch);
        let twice = seen.iter().filter(|o| o.branch == s.branch).count() > 1;
        if (!shown || twice) && !out.contains(&s.branch) {
            out.push(s.branch.clone());
        }
    }
    out
}

/// The step run of the check the workflow run waits on, if it waits on one.
pub(super) fn waiting_check(wr: &WorkflowRun) -> Option<&StepRun> {
    match (&wr.status, &wr.waiting_on, &wr.current_step) {
        (WorkflowRunStatus::Waiting, Some(WaitingOn::Check), Some(current)) => latest(wr, current),
        _ => None,
    }
}

/// The branches with the commits the workflow run recorded for them: each approval's tips and
/// each deploy's branches.
fn recorded(wr: &WorkflowRun) -> Vec<(String, String)> {
    let mut out = Vec::new();
    for s in &wr.steps {
        let approved = s
            .decision
            .as_ref()
            .filter(|d| d.get("decision").and_then(Value::as_str) == Some("approved"))
            .and_then(|d| d.get("branches"))
            .and_then(Value::as_array);
        for entry in approved.into_iter().flatten() {
            let branch = entry.get("branch").and_then(Value::as_str);
            let commit = entry.get("commit").and_then(Value::as_str);
            if let (Some(branch), Some(commit)) = (branch, commit) {
                out.push((branch.to_string(), commit.to_string()));
            }
        }
        for b in s.deploy_result.iter().flat_map(|r| &r.branches) {
            out.push((b.branch.clone(), b.commit.clone()));
        }
    }
    out
}

/// The commit at the tip of the project's default branch (`base_branch`, as deploys find it):
/// the local branch of that name, else the remote's. `None` when there is neither.
async fn default_tip(repo: &Path, wr: &WorkflowRun) -> Option<String> {
    let name = base_branch(repo, &wr.project).await?;
    let remote = format!("refs/remotes/{}/{name}", wr.project.remote.trim());
    for full in [format!("refs/heads/{name}"), remote] {
        let commit = format!("{full}^{{commit}}");
        let args = [
            "rev-parse",
            "--verify",
            "--quiet",
            "--end-of-options",
            &commit,
        ];
        if let Ok(tip) = git(repo, &args).await {
            return Some(tip);
        }
    }
    None
}

impl Workflows {
    /// What the check the workflow run `id` waits on shows (sections 5.4 and 6.2), each branch
    /// with its tip as git has it now. Read without the loop's lock: it changes nothing.
    /// `Conflict` when the run is not waiting on a check.
    pub async fn check(&self, id: &str) -> Result<CheckView> {
        let wr = self.store.get_workflow_run(id).await?;
        let Some(step_run) = waiting_check(&wr) else {
            return Err(Error::Conflict(format!(
                "workflow run {id} is not waiting for a check"
            )));
        };
        let shown = shown_at(&wr, &step_run.step_id);
        let repo = Path::new(&wr.project.repo_path);
        let mut branches = Vec::new();
        for b in &shown.branches {
            branches.push(ShownBranch {
                branch: b.branch.clone(),
                run_id: b.run_id.clone(),
                commit: tip(repo, &b.branch).await,
            });
        }
        Ok(CheckView {
            step_id: step_run.step_id.clone(),
            summary: shown.summary,
            branches,
        })
    }

    /// The committed changes of `branch` at `commit` from where it left the project's default
    /// branch (`<merge-base>..<commit>`, spec section 5.4), capped as a worktree's diff is
    /// (`workspace::MAX_DIFF_BYTES`). Only a branch the workflow run shows may be diffed: one
    /// its waiting check shows, at its tip now, or one an approval or a deploy of it recorded,
    /// at the commit recorded. `Invalid` for a branch it does not show, `Conflict` for a branch
    /// it shows at another commit, and when the default branch cannot be found.
    pub async fn branch_diff(&self, id: &str, branch: &str, commit: &str) -> Result<WorktreeDiff> {
        let wr = self.store.get_workflow_run(id).await?;
        let repo = Path::new(&wr.project.repo_path);
        let mut named = false;
        let mut at = false;
        if let Some(step_run) = waiting_check(&wr) {
            let shown = shown_at(&wr, &step_run.step_id);
            if shown.branches.iter().any(|b| b.branch == branch) {
                named = true;
                at |= tip(repo, branch).await.as_deref() == Some(commit);
            }
        }
        for (b, c) in recorded(&wr) {
            if b == branch {
                named = true;
                at |= c == commit;
            }
        }
        if !named {
            return Err(Error::Invalid(format!(
                "workflow run {id} shows no branch {branch}"
            )));
        }
        if !at {
            return Err(Error::Conflict(branches_changed(&[branch.to_string()])));
        }
        // From here on `commit` is a commit git or the run recorded, never a ref the page made.
        let Some(default) = default_tip(repo, &wr).await else {
            return Err(Error::Conflict(NO_DEFAULT_BRANCH.into()));
        };
        let base = git(repo, &["merge-base", "--end-of-options", &default, commit]).await?;
        range_diff(repo, &base, commit).await
    }
}
```

### Task 4: Workflow runs are listed by team, workflow, status and chat origin, workflows say what runs in flight, and teams refresh on dot changes

`Workflows::list` takes a `WorkflowRunFilter`, and `for_origins` finds the runs a chat started. A `Workflow` carries `runs_in_flight` and `last_run`. `Definition::validate` and `WorkflowSpec::validate` are built on new `problem` functions that name the step, and `Teams::check_workflow` gives the first problem of a draft. Renaming or deleting a dot announces its teams; a change of a team's members, and deleting a team, announce its workflows. `Runtime::workflow_webhook_url` gives the webhook's URL. Nothing in the app or the UI changes yet.

**Files:**
- Modify: `crates/dots-core/src/{model.rs,runtime.rs,teams.rs,workflow.rs,workflows.rs,store/teams.rs,store/workflow_runs.rs}`
- Test: `crates/dots-core/tests/{teams_test,store_workflow_runs_test,workflows_test,runtime_test,runtime_status_test,workflow_webhook_test}.rs`; brought up to date: `crates/dots-core/tests/{workflow_tools_test,runtime_roles_test}.rs`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: Task 1's `Store::count_workflow_runs_in_flight`; plan A's `Teams::{update, delete}`, `Workflows::delete_team`, `Runtime::{update_dot, delete_dot, webhook_url}`, `Store::teams_of_dot`.
- Produces: `model::{Workflow.runs_in_flight: u32, Workflow.last_run: Option<LastRun>, LastRun { id, status: WorkflowRunStatus, started_at, test: bool }, WorkflowRunFilter { team_id: Option<String>, workflow_id: Option<String>, statuses: Vec<WorkflowRunStatus> (empty = any), limit: Option<u32> (None = all) }}` (`Default`, `#[serde(default)]`); `WorkflowSpec::problem(&self, members: &[String]) -> Option<WorkflowProblem>`; `workflow::{WorkflowProblem { step_id: Option<String>, message: String }, Definition::problem(&self, members: &[String]) -> Option<WorkflowProblem>}` (`validate` is `problem().message`); `Teams::check_workflow(&self, team_id: &str, spec: &WorkflowSpec) -> Result<Option<WorkflowProblem>>` (no permission; `NotFound` for an unknown team); crate-private `Teams::{dot_deleted(&[String]), dot_renamed(dot_id)}`; `Workflows::{list(&self, filter: &WorkflowRunFilter) -> Result<Vec<WorkflowRun>> (was list(team_id, limit)), for_origins(&self, run_ids: &[String]) -> Result<Vec<WorkflowRun>>}`; `Runtime::workflow_webhook_url(&self, id: &str) -> Result<String>` (async; `Conflict` while the server is off, `NotFound("workflow <id>")`); `Store::{find_workflow_runs(&self, filter: &WorkflowRunFilter) -> Result<Vec<WorkflowRun>>, workflow_runs_from(&self, run_ids: &[String]) -> Result<Vec<WorkflowRun>>, last_workflow_run(&self, workflow_id: &str) -> Result<Option<LastRun>>}`; `Store::list_workflow_runs(team_id, limit)` kept as a thin wrapper.

```rust
/// The team and workflow events `events` has had since it was last read, as `team <name>` and
/// `workflow <name>` by the names in `names` (id, name).
fn team_events(
    events: &mut tokio::sync::broadcast::Receiver<RuntimeEvent>,
    names: &[(&str, &str)],
) -> Vec<String> {
    let name = |id: &str| names.iter().find(|(i, _)| *i == id).map_or("?", |(_, n)| n);
    let mut seen = Vec::new();
    while let Ok(e) = events.try_recv() {
        match e {
            RuntimeEvent::TeamUpdated { team_id } => seen.push(format!("team {}", name(&team_id))),
            RuntimeEvent::WorkflowUpdated { workflow_id, .. } => {
                seen.push(format!("workflow {}", name(&workflow_id)))
            }
            _ => {}
        }
    }
    seen
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn renaming_or_deleting_a_member_dot_announces_its_teams() {
    let t = start(None, None).await;
    let dev =
        t.rt.create_dot(folder_spec(&t.dir, "dev"), None, None)
            .await
            .unwrap();
    let helper =
        t.rt.create_dot(folder_spec(&t.dir, "helper"), None, None)
            .await
            .unwrap();
    let alone =
        t.rt.create_dot(folder_spec(&t.dir, "alone"), None, None)
            .await
            .unwrap();
    let member = |dot: &Dot| MemberSpec {
        dot_id: dot.id.clone(),
        role: "dev agent".into(),
    };
    let team = |name: &str, members: Vec<MemberSpec>| TeamSpec {
        org_id: None,
        workspace_id: "default".into(),
        name: name.into(),
        description: String::new(),
        members,
    };
    let crew =
        t.rt.teams()
            .create(&team("Crew", vec![member(&dev), member(&helper)]))
            .await
            .unwrap();
    // Teams are announced in the order of their ids, which sort by the millisecond made.
    tokio::time::sleep(std::time::Duration::from_millis(5)).await;
    let pair =
        t.rt.teams()
            .create(&team("Pair", vec![member(&dev)]))
            .await
            .unwrap();
    let fix = WorkflowSpec {
        name: "Fix".into(),
        definition: Definition {
            trigger: Trigger::Manual,
            steps: vec![Step {
                id: "a".into(),
                name: "Work".into(),
                kind: StepKind::Agent {
                    member: dev.id.clone(),
                    job: "Fix it.".into(),
                    can_send_back_to: vec![],
                    outcomes: vec![],
                    delegate: None,
                },
            }],
            budget: Budget::default(),
        },
        enabled: true,
    };
    let fix = t.rt.teams().create_workflow(&crew.id, &fix).await.unwrap();
    let names = [
        (crew.id.as_str(), "Crew"),
        (pair.id.as_str(), "Pair"),
        (fix.id.as_str(), "Fix"),
    ];
    let mut events = t.rt.subscribe();

    // A change that keeps the name announces no team.
    let mut changed = dev.spec.clone();
    changed.instructions = "Fix what you are given.".into();
    t.rt.update_dot(&dev.id, changed.clone()).await.unwrap();
    assert!(team_events(&mut events, &names).is_empty());
    // A new name does: a team shows its members by name.
    changed.name = "developer".into();
    t.rt.update_dot(&dev.id, changed).await.unwrap();
    assert_eq!(team_events(&mut events, &names), ["team Crew", "team Pair"]);
    let shown = t.rt.teams().get(&crew.id).await.unwrap();
    assert_eq!(shown.members[0].dot_name, "developer");
    // A dot in no team announces none.
    t.rt.delete_dot(&alone.id).await.unwrap();
    assert!(team_events(&mut events, &names).is_empty());
    // A deleted member leaves its teams, and their workflows may now have a problem.
    t.rt.delete_dot(&dev.id).await.unwrap();
    assert_eq!(
        team_events(&mut events, &names),
        ["team Crew", "workflow Fix", "team Pair"]
    );
    let fix = t.rt.teams().get_workflow(&fix.id).await.unwrap();
    assert_eq!(
        fix.problem,
        Some(format!(
            "step \"Work\": {} is not a member of the team",
            dev.id
        ))
    );
    t.rt.shutdown();
}
```

```rust
    WorkflowRunFilter, WorkflowRunStatus, WorkflowTrigger,
};
```
